## Assumptions (IMPORTANT!)

The operations displayed within certain frames are representative of different initial states of a cycle. There are certain periods of the activity invested into this coalition that are not cyclical and will be predeterminate of the state going into the cycle that is displayed in the mainframe![[Excalidraw/Operationalizing.md#^frame=CbNiz2JU7orG-dN8oh54T]]Thus it becomes even more important to confirm the materials are in place to facilitate certain activities. This can be an on the fly assessment of the applicability of sources on this process map or a recommendation into the initial investments into the community spaces that are currently with the coalition.
= 


## Rest of the document...
____
### Summary of cycle of permanence diagram
The cycle of permanence is a model for sustaining sustainable action via meeting a hierarchy of needs that has not been met previously in Nashville, especially not by ley people. The cycle prioritizes the production of food, medicine, living capital, and secondary forms of capital raised out of the spaces created for the sake of the production of the first three outputs of the larger system of gardens, waste processing sites, and community spaces. 

There are three types of shapes used to represent underlying intricacies and specifics in the cycle:
1. diamond nodes: states of the process; descriptive of the required conditions to measure progress.
2. circular nodes: specifics on sources that are Nashville specific. ==Simply filling out the diagram with actionable information==
3. zones: demarcations of the larger cyclical stages that are being traversed; contains nodes and arrows. 
4. arrows: action that is the transition between nodes.

### CYCLIC ALLOSTASIS AS A MODEL FOR OPERATIONALIZING

 Imagine a cycle whose arrows are clearly associated with specific tasks for which the cycle can continue, progress, or be restored from imbalance. The paradigm being laid out in this document is a visualization of coalition operations from a small to a large scale all depending on the information associated with each arrow and the statistical metrics fixed to the system (i.e. how many units are being produced and/or waste processed for production).

 Allostasis contains this ==visual metaphor== in an expected paradigm for the output and expected flow of actions in the cycle. A robust cycle will be built through the embodying of allostatic action in relation to the vital force that is being maintained in the perpetuationof said cycle and the state of capacity formed out of circular completion. Similar to allostasis, there are different modes of action: continuation, scaling/growth/de-growth, correction, cyclical shift (rare; heavy disruption of cycle). These will be operationalized as well into a task that is legible and flexible

 The outline here described ensures that for every action, there is a reaction and outcome that can be acted upon. ==True mastery over this model in its implementation will rely on the expansion of the information contained within each arrow: ==
 - For each arrow, what are the associated nodes (input/output beginning/end) and the change between these nodes?
 - What are the material, social, spiritual, cultural, technological, financial, or interpersonal circumstances that must be managed along an arrow?
 - Expected time, skills, labor time, and number of people responsible for each arrow (preferably within an adjustable ratio that can be measured and tuned over time in practice)
 - What kind of action is the arrow taking within the allostatic framework? ==This is of particular importance in ascertaining the urgency of the action and how it is going to affect arrows downstream==

### Legend

![[Excalidraw/Operationalizing.md#^group=x8p5y4UrOqQc9ZFyNp7QX]]
1. Ovals: Source of motion/resources in the process. material and resource focus
2. Arrows: refer to section on cyclic allostasis metaphor; actions and tasks to operationalize.
3. Diamonds: State of material flows/stores. Might be accompanied with smaller diamonds that can give more details on the state of the process?

List of potential disruptions in the cycle of permanence: 
1. Heavy metal poisoning or poisoning via other chemical agents
(insecticides, pesticides, herbicides, extreme nutritive leeching)
2. Climate change
3. Military intervention (harder to account for; very short timescale)
4. Shifting waste streams/encapsulated waste streams
5. Coalition fracture
6. Breakdown of municipal supporting structures (apocalyptic scenario)
